# Open Threads — LIVE, Volume 08 (*What Holds Without a Name*, Chapters 341–390) closed, on top of Volumes 05 to 07

**Scope of this file: Volume 05 onward, with Volume 08 closed at Chapter 390.** Everything before Chapter 201 was rotated to `state/archive/open-threads-through-volume-04.md` on the Batch 0005 review repair, verbatim, because the file had reached 204,592 bytes and a phase could no longer load it. Nothing was lost.

**THE HEADER ON THE LINE ABOVE SAID VOLUME 05 UNTIL THE REVIEW OF THE VOLUME 08 CLOSE CAUGHT IT, THREE VOLUMES STALE, AND IT IS CORRECTED HERE.** Nothing below it was rewritten, because the sections above are the record of their own blocks and the sections at the end are the live ones. **AND THIS FILE IS 330 KB AND MUST NOT BE LOADED WHOLE: read it newest section first — section 22 — and treat `state/volume-08-roll-summary.md` as the volume index, which is what it is.**

**The sections below are in chronological order, oldest first, and the last section of each batch is the one that describes its final chapter.**

# Volume 05 threads — Batch 0001, Chapters 201 to 210

**Added, not replacing. The Volume 04 threads above are still open and are carried forward. Nothing in this section closes a Volume 04 thread and nothing in it opens a new final enemy.**

## Opened in this block and live

- **THE PLACE THAT THE LEASE IS ON IS NOT NAMED AND WAS NOT NAMED ON ANY DAY OF THIS BLOCK.** A lease is renewed on a road; the toll is paid at the ninth place; the holder is the Registrar-General, Cael Orin; the place is named on the lease and this is not the lease. **The Registry man refused the place three times and never once refused outright, and on the seventh he said he had been sitting on a bench for four nights working out how to be given a road to a place he would not name, and had not managed it.** Nothing in this block says where the road goes. **A document which says what it will not say is a door, and this district settled that on the twenty-seventh of December and has now got one in a stranger's hand.** 202, 203, 207, 208.

- **THE NINTH PLACE TAKES A TOLL AND IS NOT A ROAD, AND NOBODY KNOWS WHAT IT IS.** The ninth row of the stranger's schedule carries a word where the other eight carry a day, and the word is IT IS NOT A ROAD. **A word in a column headed OPEN is a finding and not a blank.** The toll on the lease is paid there. **The ninth holding on the delivery sheet at the second reservoir is the one nobody can account for and has been since the second of January, refused five times in eleven weeks by a clerk of a house and a sixth time on the fourth of April, and a man of about thirty at the counter has been down that road eleven times in eight weeks.** **The two are a rhyme and have been refused as a join on the page twice, in Chapter 205 and again in Chapter 209, and a resemblance noticed a second time is not a finding and a third time is a habit.** 205, 208, 209.

- **EIGHT OF NINE THINGS ARE STANDING OPEN ON EIGHT NIGHTS INSIDE ELEVEN DAYS, ONE A NIGHT, AND THE NINTH HAS A WORD INSTEAD OF A DAY.** The days are the first, second, third, fourth, sixth, eighth, tenth and eleventh of April, and the fifth, seventh and ninth carry nothing. **Nobody in the Uplands opened them and nobody in this basin has said why. The run does not stop at eight, because it is the eighth.** **The last of the eight is the eleventh of April, which is the last day of Batch 0001.** A ninth night with no day on it is owed and nobody in this district can say when. 209, 210.

- **FOUR GENERATIONS OF PEOPLE WHO KEEP THEM HAVE NEVER BEEN ON A PAGE OF ANYBODY'S, AND THIS DISTRICT HAS NO INSTRUMENT FOR ASKING ANYBODY A DAY'S WALK NORTH.** The figure came from a stranger, with no date, no year and no place attached. **A keeper is a person who is on a hill and is not on a page; a Registry does not keep keepers; a lease says what the holder may do with a thing and does not say who keeps it.** **Nobody in this district has ever seen a keeper, and the man who keeps a road in this district is not one of them and has not been sent for.** 207, 209.

- **A DRAY WENT UP THE ONLY ROAD THAT CLIMBS ON THE ELEVENTH OF APRIL WITH A STRANGER IN IT AND A YOKE LASHED TO THE TAILBOARD, AND THIS BLOCK SAYS NOTHING ABOUT WHERE IT IS OR WHEN IT COMES BACK.** The lease has to be answered at the place on the last day of the fourth month, which is nineteen days off at the end of Chapter 210. **The man of about thirty-one drove it. The Registry man is on a road he will not name with a case under his arm.** The two hundred yards of soft grade is the thing the dray cannot do. 208, 209, 210.

- **A JOB WAS TAKEN OFF A WOMAN'S STONES IN A YARD AND THE YARD WAS THE INSTRUMENT.** The district built a book a stranger can walk up to and called it a door and told nineteen people it was the best instrument in the district, and **a yard that about nineteen people can stand in and decide something about a woman who is not in it is also a door, and the door that got used this month was not the one anybody was proud of.** **A piece of wood on a cart that came out of a yard is a job, and the job is hers because it came off her stones, and she is not in the cart and she is not in the yard. A job taken is still a job and the person it was taken from is still the person who does it.** **Nobody has priced a day of anything she does, and a woman who says the same true thing three times in eleven days is not a woman who has run out of things to say; she is a woman who has run out of people who can price one day.** 208, 209, 210.

- **THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE THINGS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT HAVE IS STILL A WAY TO PAY A PERSON WHO IS NOT IN A HOUSEHOLD, AND THIS BLOCK NAMED IT AGAIN THREE TIMES.** A woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale offered four houses of a round of about ninety on the thirty-first of the third month and the offer still stands and has not been taken up. **Four houses out of about ninety is not a round and is not going to become a round. A yoke is not four houses and is not a round and cannot become either. A man of about thirty-one has not got a way to pay a woman for two days and said so out loud in a yard in fifty words.** 201, 208, 209, 210.

- **A MAN HAS BEEN FOUND AND NOT RELEASED.** A man of about thirty-one walked two days of road to a counter, had his own name taken off a sheet in about nine seconds at no cost, and came home on the third of April with a strip of paper about the length of his hand that says the name came off. **He said in fifty-one words that he has not been released and has been found. A name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on, and what came off here is not the figure and is the name, and the figure of twenty-one years came off this district's own page on the thirty-first of March in the same line as the date and was not asked for by anybody.** **The two removals are eleven inches and two days of road apart and neither of them is a release and the two are not joined.** He said it can be taken off where they put it on and not by him. **He does not know what a person at the back of a book is for.** 203, 205, 208, 209.

- **A REGISTRY HAS READ A PAGE IN THIS DISTRICT AND WILL NOT SAY WHERE IT GOT A NAME.** A man of about thirty at the counter said on the sixth of April, in four seconds, that his house had never heard of a Registry. **The house thirty-four miles east and the Registry are two bodies and neither of them has heard of the other, and the day they meet they will meet over a place with no name on a sheet, and neither of them has a reason to be careful about the other yet.** The fourth of the five posted securities is a receipt in a building a stranger may read and may not walk off with, and the two are a rhyme and have not been joined. 202, 206, 207.

- **THE REGISTRAR SAID IN A LANE ON THE SEVENTH THAT HE IS NOT A STRANGER AND WOULD NOT SAY THE REST OF IT.** Thirty-two words, no clerk within nine feet, nobody counting. **What was said between two people on the seventh of April is in a minute in her own words if she chooses to write it and is not a document this district can produce if she does not.** The two of them have been in the same yard at the same hour on purpose nine times since the eighteenth of December and this was not one of the nine. **A slate went past a kitchen in Alder Reach eleven times last month and neither of them wrote on it and it is not to be asked about.** 207.

- **THE ONLY SENTENCE SAID ABOUT A DRAY GOING UP A ROAD WAS NOT WRITTEN DOWN.** A man of about thirty-four who digs loam asked a man of fifty-six to write it in his own words and could not, because a man of fifty-six is not a clerk and has said so about himself eleven times in four months, and nobody else wrote it. 210.

- **THE EIGHT DAYS ARE IN A BOOK NOW.** Eight printed days went into the registrar's office on the tenth of April, read back and got right, **and she said in sixty-one words that a day a stranger has printed is a day somebody is going to be told about and a day in a book is a day a person can be asked about, and that this basin has never had a book in which a printed day could be found by anybody who wanted to find it.** **This is the first page in this basin a stranger could use to find a day, and it is a door, and it was the district's own decision.** It is also the first instrument of the volume that anybody in the yard argued for. 210.

## Carried forward unspent from Volume 04 and untouched in this block

- **The midpoint reversal** — a disclosure showing that one of his own secure lots has been assigning unpaid labour to the registrar's registry, disclosed by the man of about thirty-one who mends a dray because a Registry put a name on a page and the name was his and he read it in a room with the registrar in it. **NOT SPENT. It is Volume 05's midpoint and the volume outline puts it at Chapters 224 to 227, and nothing in Batch 0001 spends it.** **The man is on a road with a stranger in this block and has not been in a room with the registrar since the fourth of the fourth month.**
- **The Lock Council, the Upland assemblies, the Guilds, the record caves, the founder's chair, the nine design signatures and the ninth of them.** Not in this block and not named.
- **The Lock Council's first letter to Alder Reach** does not exist yet. It is Batch 0002's.
- **Cael Orin and Adrian have not met and do not meet in this block.** **Orin's name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was not written down, and a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said that a name said once in a yard is a name four hundred people have and a name said twice is a name a counter can ask for.**
- **The ninth design signature belongs to Iven Tallow and Iven is not in this volume.** Untouched.
- **The office** — five places, no register, eleven trades unable to state its function, two valid certificates in a drawer thirty-four miles away. **Not approached in any of the ten chapters.**
- **The fourth line of the offer** has not been read out loud. The refusals to read are nine and the departure is unspent and there is still no date in the ledger for the next reading.
- **A rate that turns a year into coppers.** There is none and there never has been one, and it is restated in Chapter 210.
- **The two figures of five years and nine years.** Not joined and neither can be got off the other.
- **The boy of nine and his name in a column in a building two days of road away.** Nobody has ever told the boy that the house's own counter says a man may take his name off a sheet. **In Chapter 203 a man of about twenty-three read the second line of that sheet out in a lane for the first time, which is the day at the bottom of it, the twenty-ninth of December, and it is not on the copy the family was given.**

## Corrections to the Volume 05 Batch 0001 threads, from the review repair

**No thread was opened or closed by the repair, no thread above was moved, and no new final enemy was introduced. The full account is `state/volume-05-batch-0001-summary.md` at section 9.24 to 9.34, and the findings it answers are in `logs/next-0001.review.log` under *Review: phase next-0001*.**

- **THE NINTH OF THE NINE HOLDINGS, AND THE COUNT OF REFUSALS, WERE TWO FIGURES RUN TOGETHER AND ARE NOW KEPT APART.** **The pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given is SEVEN, not six.** It reached six in Volume 04, on the twenty-fourth of the third month in Chapter 198, and the seventh was given on the fourth of April in Chapter 204. **The refusals about the ninth holding by the man of about thirty are a separate figure: five through the third of April, six from the fourth.** The prose said five, six, five across Chapters 203, 204 and 205, and every state file had frozen the count at six because they had all taken it from Chapter 204. **The count that arrived already full was the pool, not the ninth holding, and no count checked only inside this block could have seen it.**
- **Nine intervals in the block were stock phrases and not figures.** *Eleven weeks* and *eight weeks* stood in for **six weeks and four days, seven weeks and three days, twelve weeks and six days, thirteen weeks, thirteen weeks and a day, twenty-three weeks, ninety-one days and ninety-three days**, and for the **twelfth and thirteenth** times down the eleven mile road, where Volume 04's Chapter 199 had already put him at the tenth on the twenty-seventh of the third month. **All nine were too short and all nine are now counted off the day their own sentence names.**
- **A STAY IS COUNTED WITHOUT THE DAY OF ARRIVAL, AND A RUN OF DAYS THAT INCLUDES IT SAYS SO.** The man with the portfolio came in on the second of April; the seventh was his fifth day, the eighth his sixth, the ninth his seventh, the tenth his eighth, the eleventh his ninth. **He slept five nights on a bench, the nights of the second to the sixth, and six more on a dray.** The Registry's borrowed word is four days in Chapter 205, seven in Chapter 208 and eight in Chapter 209, being the second of April counted in. **The two conventions are now stated once in the canon card and must not be mixed inside one sentence.**
- **THE BLOCK CARRIES TWO SYSTEM PANELS AND NOT THREE,** at Chapters 207 and 210, on the test Volume 04's close states: a run of `> **` that is not introduced as a sheet somebody reads out. **Six such runs are in the block and four of them are printed documents — a lease, a stranger's schedule, a clerk's list and eight days read off a copy.**
- **Chapter 209's woman was being put four miles away and on a hill at the same time.** The counter she filled her buckets at is **at the foot of the bank**, which is what the arithmetic always said. The sluice end of the loams is untouched in Chapters 201 and 203.
- **THE REGISTRAR'S REFUSALS IN THIS BLOCK ARE TWO AND HER GRANT IS ONE.** The refusal on the tenth of April, in thirty-two words, of being made to walk the yoke down the bank is **the woman of fifty-eight's** and not hers, and the canon card had it as hers until this repair. **The romance is unresolved and nothing here moved it.**

# Volume 05 Batch 0002 — open threads, Chapters 211 to 220

**The block's finding, once: two lists of nine that agree on eight are not one list, and the one they disagree about is the ninth, and the ninth is a word in a stranger's hand and the twentieth of April in a body's own hand.** **The block's second finding, and it answers a question the phase prompt left open: no keeper has ever been on a page of anybody's, and it is not because pages forget people, it is because the page has a column and the column carries a house.**

## Opened or carried in this block

- **THE NINTH OF THE NINE IS A WORD IN ONE DOCUMENT AND THE TWENTIETH OF APRIL IN THE OTHER, AND THE TWENTIETH HAS COME AND GONE AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT CAN SAY WHETHER ANYTHING HAPPENED.** The stranger's schedule carries eight days and IT IS NOT A ROAD in the ninth row. The keepers' own standing orders carry nine nights and the ninth is the twentieth of April. **A copy of the standing orders came down on a dray on the fourteenth of April and was read out in a yard the same afternoon, and the two-column comparison with the road house's book was read back in Chapter 214 and reproduces.** 213, 214, 218, 219, 220. **OPEN AND LOAD-BEARING.**

- **THE COLUMN HEADED KEEPER CARRIES A HOUSE NINE TIMES AND A PERSON NO TIMES.** The keepers' page has been doing this district's own rule for four generations, and a Registry does it, and this district's own records office does it. **A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said in ninety-six words on the fourteenth that no keeper has ever been on a page of anybody's and that it is not because pages forget people, it is because the page has a column and the column carries a house.** 213, 214, 220. **OPEN.** The next question is what a page that names a house can be asked, and the caves give a page with nine names and one rule on it and that is Batch 0003's.

- **THE ROAD HOUSE'S BOOK IS IN MORE THAN ONE HAND AND THE MAN WHO KEEPS THAT ROAD REFUSED THE NINTH IN FORTY-TWO WORDS WITH A REASON.** *That one is a night another man wrote and not a night I walked, and I keep a road and I am not a keeper of a book, and I am not taking a man's page round a yard like this one.* **A refusal with a reason, so it is not one of the refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given, and the count of eight did not move because of it.** 212. **OPEN.** The ninth is on a page somewhere and the man who holds it is nine miles off.

- **TWO WORDS FOR A MAN AND NO NAME FOR HIM.** The man who keeps that road said his house has a word for the man who turns one and a word for the night he turns it and that neither of those two words is on any page. The man of about thirty at the counter said his house has never printed a keeper's name on anything and he has asked about it twice in thirty-four miles and nobody would tell him one. **A district with two words for a man and no name for him has got a word and a word is not a figure and is not a document.** 212, 214, 220. **OPEN.**

- **THE FIRST LETTER THIS DISTRICT HAS HAD FROM A COUNCIL ASKS FOR A MAN BY THE LAST DAY OF THE MONTH, AND THE LAST DAY OF THE MONTH IS NINE DAYS OFF AT THE END OF CHAPTER 220, AND THE LEASE HAS TO BE ANSWERED AT A PLACE WITH NO NAME ON IT ON THAT SAME DAY, AND THIS DISTRICT CAN ANSWER NEITHER.** The letter was read out in a yard on the sixteenth of April and was read and not discussed. **The yard's finding: a body that writes to a village is a body that knows the village has something, and it has known for four months because it apologises for the first of these coming four months late, and nobody in this basin is going to tell me that a body apologises for a thing it has not counted.** **The man the figure of twenty-one years is against refused in seventy-eight words to have the district's answer decided by a yard.** 215, 218, 220. **OPEN AND THE BLOCK'S FORWARD PULL.** The Council's chair is Batch 0003's.

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WENT UP THE ROAD AT ABOUT HALF PAST FIVE ON THE TWENTIETH WITH A YOKE LEFT IN THE FORGE AND WAS NOT BACK ON THE TWENTY-FIRST, AND THE STRIP OF PAPER IS BACK ON A TABLE IN THIS DISTRICT.** He came down on the fourteenth with the yoke and a printed sheet under it and the second man who went up with him was not on it and the case was not on it. **Nothing in this block says where the road goes, and a man who has been found and not released is on a road in it.** 219, 220. **OPEN.**

- **THE FOURTH HOLDING TOOK ITS FIRST DROP SINCE THE FIRST OF THE SECOND MONTH AND THE CHANNEL AT THE HEAD OF THE TOP FIELD HAS ABOUT A FOOT OF WATER IN IT, AND THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM CALLED THAT A THIRD RESEMBLANCE AND A HABIT.** A tank four miles down a lane came up within a hand's breadth of its lip on the twentieth for the second time since the first of the second month and a clerk entered that a figure which describes a butt does not describe a night nine miles off. **It is not joined to the road a route keeper says somebody opened on the night of the twenty-second of the second month, and it is not joined to the ninth of the nine nights, and both refusals are on the page.** 219, 220. **REFUSED AS A JOIN AND TO BE LEFT REFUSED.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING ASKED THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT HIMSELF, IN HIS OWN WORDS, ON THE SIXTEENTH OF APRIL, AFTER SAYING ON THE EIGHTH THAT HE WOULD AND NOT DOING IT ON THE NINTH.** The thread Batch 0001 handed over is closed on the page and the reason it is closed is that he named the day in a yard and the day arrived. **She said eleven and refused in seventy-three words to have the eleven names said in a yard.** 215. **KEPT.**

- **THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT HOLDS ELEVEN HOUSES AND NO FIGURE AND STARTED AT SEVEN IN THE MORNING ON THE TWENTY-FIRST.** The round of about seventy-nine is not coming back and about ninety is not a figure so that is not a subtraction. **The offer of four houses from the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale still stands, has not been taken up, and four and eleven were refused entry as an addition on the page.** **Nobody has priced a day of any of it and the fifth of the five things this district does not have did not move.** 216, 219, 220. **OPEN AND UNPRICED.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM HAS ASKED THREE TIMES IN TEN DAYS FOR A SENTENCE OF HIS OWN TO BE WRITTEN DOWN AND HAS NOT BEEN WRITTEN DOWN THREE TIMES.** The eleventh of April, the nineteenth and the twenty-first, the last time because a man of fifty-six is not a clerk. **He also said on the nineteenth that he has disagreed with one panel in fourteen days and has not disagreed with one, and that the one he has not disagreed with is the one about a person, and that he was not going to say any more about that.** 211, 218, 220. **OPEN.**

- **THE COUNT OF REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN MOVED FROM SEVEN TO EIGHT ON THE FIFTEENTH OF APRIL, ON THE PAGE, FOR THE FIRST TIME SINCE THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF THE MONTH BEFORE LAST.** The refusals about the ninth holding are at SEVEN and the pool is at EIGHT and **the two are not the same figure and are not added together.** 214, 215, 217, 218, 220. **OPEN, AND A NINTH MUST NOT BE ENTERED WITHOUT A REASON ON THE PAGE.**

- **THE ROMANCE MOVED ONCE AND IS UNRESOLVED.** In a lane behind the forge end off Salt Row on the eighteenth of April, with no clerk within nine feet, **the registrar asked him for the page of the ward market's own book where the figure of twenty-one years came off, and he gave her a different page**, which carries the date and his own hand and nothing written under it. **Nobody counted either sentence. He refused nothing and she granted nothing. They have been in the same yard at the same hour on purpose nine times since the eighteenth of December and neither sentence is one of the nine.** 217. **UNRESOLVED, AND THE VOLUME MUST NOT END ON A SETTLEMENT.**

- **A MAN WHO KEEPS A ROAD WALKS IT WHEN THERE IS WATER IN A CHANNEL, AND THAT IS A REASON AND IS NOT A KEEPER'S REASON, AND HE WOULD KNOW.** 212. **OPEN.**

## Carried forward unspent from Batch 0001 and untouched in this block

- **The midpoint reversal** — a disclosure showing that one of his own posted securities has been assigning unpaid labour to the registrar's registry, disclosed by the man of about thirty-one who mends a dray because a Registry put a name on a page and the name was his and he read it in a room with the registrar in it. **NOT SPENT. It is Chapters 224 to 227. The man has not been in a room with the registrar since the fourth of April and this block deliberately left the strip of paper on a trestle table in a yard with nobody reading it, so that the disclosure has somewhere to happen.**
- **The record caves, the founder's chair, the nine design signatures, the tolls arriving in villages that did not sign, the nine local charters, the regional covenant and the ninth signature.** Not in this block.
- **Cael Orin and Adrian have not met and do not meet in this block. Orin's name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was NOT said a second time in Chapters 211 to 220**, and the yard's own rule about a name said twice was used in Chapter 215 by a woman of fifty-eight refusing to say eleven housekeepers' names.
- **The ninth design signature belongs to Iven Tallow and Iven is not in this volume.** Untouched and not foreshadowed.
- **The office** — five places, no register, eleven trades unable to state its function, two valid certificates in a drawer thirty-four miles away. **Not approached in any of the ten chapters.**
- **The fourth line of the offer** has not been read out loud. The refusals to read are nine, the departure is unspent, and there is still no date in the ledger for the next reading.
- **A rate that turns a year into coppers.** There is none and there never has been one, and it is restated in Chapters 213, 216, 218 and 220.
- **The two figures of five years and nine years.** Not joined and neither can be got off the other.
- **The boy of nine and his name in a column in a building two days of road away.** Untouched.
- **Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block and nothing required him.**

## Corrections this block made, that a Batch 0003 writer must inherit

- **THE ORDINAL FOR THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER IS FIFTEEN, NOT FOURTEEN.** The phase prompt and the Batch 0001 record's section 10.1 both said he was at the thirteenth and that the next one was the fourteenth, and that was a figure carried in a state file from Chapter 206 and it did not carry the ninth of April. **Chapter 204 puts him at the twelfth on the fourth, Chapter 206 at the thirteenth on the sixth, and Chapter 209 has him down the road on the ninth, which is the fourteenth. Chapter 214 makes him the fifteenth, names all four earlier visits with their days, and derives the interval off the second of January: a hundred and three days, which is fourteen weeks and five days.** The next one is the **sixteenth** and no chapter fixes a day for it, because this district has no schedule for him. **Any state file that says thirteenth, fourteenth or fifteenth for him is wrong; the chain is eleventh, twelfth, thirteenth, fourteenth, fifteenth.**
- **THE STALE BASE OF 96 FOR THE UNENTERED DAYS IS STILL 31 DAYS OUT AND WAS NOT USED.** The base is 127 at the last day of the third month and the next figure is 127 plus the day of the fourth month: 139 on the twelfth of April and 148 on the twenty-first.
- **TWO PANELS IN THIS BLOCK, NOT THREE, and the cap for the next block is two.** Four of the six runs of `> **` are printed documents and are not panels.
- **THE RELATIVE MONTH IS *LAST MONTH* AND IT MEANS THE THIRD MONTH, AND A BATCH 0003 WRITER MUST NOT UNDO THE REPAIR.** Chapters 211 to 220 said *the month before last* in eighteen places and meant the third month, which from the fourth month is *last month*; Chapter 215's *the thirty-first of the month before last* was a date that does not exist, because the figure of twenty-one years came off this district's own page on the thirty-first of the third month. **All eighteen now read *last month*, and the pool of refusals with no reason last moved on the twenty-fourth of last month, which is the third month, which is twenty-two days before the fifteenth of April.** `state/current.md` was already right and is unchanged: the current month is the fourth, last month is the third, the month before last is the second.
- **THE KEEPER'S COLUMN IS HEADED WITH A WORD AND NOT WITH A NAME, in Chapters 213, 218 and 220.** The standing orders print a column headed KEEPER and carry a house under it nine times and a person no times; three sentences had described the heading as a keeper's name, which asserts the opposite of the block's own finding. **The counted speech in Chapter 213 is ninety-six words and was ninety-seven, because the phrase was corrected and the claim was recounted.**
- **TWO HOUSES HAVE NOW GIVEN THIS DISTRICT THE SAME PAIR OF WORDS, and the thread is 209, 212 and 220.** The man of about thirty at a counter gave the pair on the ninth of April and the man who keeps a road gave the same pair out of a different house on the thirteenth; the Chapter 212 line that said the counter man did not give the other two was false and is repaired, and the *neither is on any page* half of it belongs to the road man, not to the counter man.
- **A BLOCK RECORD MAY NOT DEFINE AWAY A FALSE COUNT IN THE PROSE.** Chapter 220's *three days running* was wrong and the Batch 0002 writing pass defended it at 9.5 with three days that were not on the page. **A self-counting character may be wrong and a clerk may catch him; a state file may not be the thing that excuses him.** See `state/volume-05-batch-0002-summary.md` at 9.5 and at 11.

# Volume 05 Batch 0003 — open threads, Chapters 221 to 230

**The block's finding, once: the instrument that failed in this block was the disclosure and not the secrecy, because a name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on, so the man who said it out loud made the name harder to get rid of and lost nothing that was his. The block's second finding: nine people wrote a rule four hundred years ago that says no person shall hold this alone, and one of them sat down afterwards and wrote that a man thanked for it has been made something else, and the something is not a chair.**

## Opened or carried in this block

- **THE COUNCIL OFFERS ONE HOLDER, ONE CHAIR AND A RULED PLACE FOR A NAME, AND IT IS REFUSED TWICE, IN A ROOM AND IN A YARD, AND THE TWO REFUSALS ARE NOT THE SAME REFUSAL.** The sheet is dated the eighteenth of April, lay on a trestle table on the twenty-second with nobody in the yard having put it there, and was read out and read back. **A chair stood in that yard from the twenty-third to the twenty-fourth and nobody sat in it and it was moved four feet once.** The room refusal is on the ground that a single holder cannot be asked a question by anybody; the yard refusal is on the ground that a yard is not a body. 221, 222, 223, 227, 229. **OPEN, ANSWERED AND NOT WITHDRAWN — the answer went up the road on the first of the fifth month and nothing has come back down it.** **The word founder is in this volume now and it came on the Council's paper, and a man in the yard said on the twenty-eighth that he will not be a founder because a yard is full.**

- **THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL IS SPENT, IN CHAPTERS 224 TO 227, AND IT IS NOT TO BE SPENT AGAIN.** A page in a Registry's hand carries the name of the man of about thirty-one against this district's records office, entered on the second of April, the day he gave a name at a counter that takes names off sheets, and it says he serves that office. He has done the work nine or ten times, was never given the job, and was never paid a penny of it. **He read it on the twenty-second in a room with the registrar in it and said nothing for three days, and then disclosed it himself, in a yard on the twenty-sixth and in a room with a shut door in it on the night of the twenty-fifth.** He loses nothing that was his and pays for saying it: the labour stops, the name is findable where it was only askable, and **the name cannot be taken off by him.** 224, 225, 226, 227. **SPENT.**

- **A THIRD COLUMN HEADED WITH A WORD AND NOT A NAME, AND IT CARRIES A MAN ONCE.** The first carries a house nine times and a person no times and has for four generations. **A column is a column and a man who is in one is not in it because anybody asked him.** 225, 226. **OPEN, and it is the same structure as this district's own rule about naming people, which is why the loam man calls a column a column.**

- **THE WORK DID NOT STOP, IT MOVED.** A ledger of nineteen rows and four columns, about as much as a full bucket, on a table four feet high under a hatch at the end of a lane, two hundred yards each way, on the four days a month a man of about nineteen is not well. It has not gone since the twenty-second of April. Nobody has been asked to take it. **A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat carried one on the twenty-second without knowing it was a job and asked for the not-asking to be written down and it was written down and it is not a column.** 226, 227. **OPEN, AND THE REVERSAL RUNNING.**

- **THE REGISTRAR MAY NOT CARRY THE PAGE OUT OF HER OWN OFFICE, BECAUSE THE RULE IS HERS AND SHE WROTE IT ON THE TWENTY-FIRST OF THIS MONTH BEFORE SHE KNEW THERE WAS ANYTHING ON IT, AND SHE ENTERED FOUR LINES THAT DO NOT CARRY THE NAME.** 225. **OPEN. A shut room is a place where a sentence goes to die and not a book, and this block's most honest disclosure produced no page at all.**

- **A HOUSE NINE MILES UP WILL LET A PAGE BE COPIED AND WILL NOT LET A PAGE BE CARRIED, AND IT HAS KEPT A THING IN THE WATER FOR FOUR GENERATIONS AND CANNOT BE ASKED WHO KEPT IT.** 228. **No keeper has ever been on a page and there never was one, and the page carries the house. OPEN, and it is the only way into the Uplands anybody in this district has.**

- **A PAGE IN A CAVE WITH NINE PLACES ON IT, EIGHT NAMES ON EIGHT OF THEM, ONE RULE SAYING NO PERSON SHALL HOLD THIS ALONE, AND A NINTH PLACE THAT IS RULED AND EMPTY.** **The safeguard was designed to distribute power and not to crown anybody, and one of the nine wrote underneath it, in a second hand, one sentence underlined twice: A MAN WHO IS THANKED FOR THIS HAS BEEN MADE SOMETHING ELSE, AND THE SOMETHING IS NOT A CHAIR.** **The eight names were not read out in the doorway and were not read out in a yard.** 228, 230. **OPEN AND LOAD-BEARING. THE NINTH NAME IS NOT ON THE PAGE AND IS THE VOLUME'S NEXT QUESTION AND BELONGS TO IVEN TALLOW, WHO IS NOT IN THE VOLUME, DOES NOT APPEAR, AND IS NOT TO BE NAMED OR FORESHADOWED.**

- **THE NINTH PLACE ON THE CAVE PAGE IS THE SAME SHAPE OF NOTHING AS THE SEVENTH LINE AT THE NINTH PLACE ON THE BOARD OUTSIDE THE ROOM, AND A MAN SAID SO OUT LOUD BEFORE ANYBODY ELSE DID, TWICE, ON THE TWENTY-NINTH OF APRIL AND AGAIN ON THE SECOND OF THE FIFTH MONTH.** 228, 230. **OPEN, and it is the volume's shape of nothing finding its second instance and it must not be treated as a join.**

- **THE MONTH TURNED IN THE NIGHT OF THE THIRTIETH OF APRIL AND WAS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE, ON THE SECOND OF THE FIFTH MONTH, WITH THE MONTH BEFORE IT NAMED AS THE FOURTH AND THE MONTH BEFORE THAT AS THE THIRD, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT THE YARD WAS AT THE COUNTER AT THE LAST HOUR OF THE LAST DAY OF THE LAST MONTH.** 229, 230. **SAID ONCE. DO NOT SAY IT AGAIN.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM ASKED FOUR TIMES IN ELEVEN DAYS FOR A SENTENCE OF HIS OWN TO BE WRITTEN DOWN AND WAS NOT WRITTEN DOWN FOUR TIMES, AND ON THE THIRTIETH, WITHOUT ASKING, HE WAS WRITTEN DOWN, AND HE SAID IT IS NOT THE SAME THING AND THAT IT IS THE BEST THAT HAS HAPPENED TO HIM IN A MONTH AND HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY THANK YOU IN FRONT OF NINE PEOPLE.** 221, 229. **OPEN AND NOT A RESOLUTION.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM PUT A SPADE INTO A WET BANK ON THE TWENTY-SECOND AND COULD NOT LIFT A SPADE ABOVE HIS HEAD FOR THE REST OF THE BLOCK, CARRIED A LAMP UP A FOUR-MILE CLIMB IN HIS RIGHT HAND ON THE TWENTY-NINTH AND COULD NOT GET HIS FINGERS TO CLOSE ON IT, AND GOT THE SPADE ABOVE HIS HEAD IN TWO GOES ON THE SECOND OF THE FIFTH MONTH.** 221, 228, 230. **OPEN AS A BODY, NOT AS A FIGURE.**

- **THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT HOLDS ELEVEN HOUSES AND NO FIGURE, SAID IN SEVENTY-FIVE WORDS ON THE TWENTY-SECOND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHICH OF TWO THINGS SHE HAS BEEN FOR NINE YEARS IN FRONT OF NINE PEOPLE, USED THE YARD'S OWN RULE ABOUT A NAME SAID TWICE ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH TO PROTECT A MAN WHO IS NOT HERS, REFUSED IN THIRTY-NINE WORDS TO HEAR A LETTER ABOUT A CHAIR READ OUT, AND ON THE SECOND OF THE FIFTH MONTH SAID THAT A THING SHE HAS DONE HAS GONE UNWRITTEN FOR THE FIRST TIME IN NINE YEARS AND THAT SHE DOES NOT MIND.** 221, 226, 227, 230. **OPEN AND UNPRICED. The eleventh bucket went up the bank at about ten past twelve on the second and nobody counted the buckets. The round of about seventy-nine is not coming back. The offer of four houses from the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale still stands, has not been taken up, and four and eleven were refused entry as an addition on the page.**

- **THE LEASE WAS NOT ANSWERED ON THE DAY IT FELL DUE AND THE ANSWER TO THE COUNCIL WENT UP THE ONLY ROAD IN THIS DISTRICT THAT CLIMBS WITH A MAN ON THE BOX WHO WAS GOING UP ANYWAY, AND THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST WROTE IT HIMSELF AND SIGNED IT.** 229. **OPEN. The man with the portfolio is still not in this district and nothing in this block says where he is.**

- **THE TANK FOUR MILES DOWN A LANE IS OVER ITS LIP, THERE ARE TWO CHALK LINES ON THE INSIDE OF THE BRICK, NINE YEARS OF BEDS ARE IN A SECOND ROOM, THE CRACK IS RUNNING, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED FOR A FIFTH MEND.** 224, 229. **OPEN, and it is not joined to the ninth of the nine nights or to the channel at the head of the top field and both refusals are on the page.**

- **THE POOL OF REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN STOOD AT EIGHT BECAUSE A YARD CHOSE NOT TO ASK, IN NINETY-FIVE WORDS, ON THE TWENTY-THIRD.** 222. **OPEN, AND A NINTH MUST NOT BE ENTERED WITHOUT A REASON ON THE PAGE. The refusals about the ninth holding are at SEVEN. The man of about thirty at a counter is at the sixteenth time and the next is the seventeenth.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER GAVE FOUR WORDS AT A GATE AS HE WAS LEAVING AND NOBODY HEARD THEM AND A MAN WHO MENDS FENCING ENTERED THEM THE NEXT MORNING: A HOUSE AT A ROAD NINE MILES UP WILL LET A PAGE BE COPIED AND WILL NOT LET A PAGE BE CARRIED.** He had asked twice in thirty-four miles and had never been given an answer and had stopped asking, and he gave a word and not a name. 222, 228. **OPEN, and it is the first time a thread paid out through a counter man rather than through a document.**

## Carried forward unspent from Batch 0001 and Batch 0002, and untouched in this block

- **The tolls arriving in villages that did not sign, the nine local charters, the regional covenant, the Council's letter calling this district's man a founder on nine walls, and the Convener promotion.** Not in this block. Chapters 231 onward.
- **Cael Orin and the man the figure of twenty-one years is against have not met and did not meet in this block, and Orin's name was not said a third time.**
- **The ninth design signature belongs to Iven Tallow and Iven is not in this volume.** Untouched and not foreshadowed.
- **The office** — five places, no register, two valid certificates in a drawer thirty-four miles away. **Not approached in any of the ten chapters**, and the records office in this block is a different room and has been one since the fourth of December.
- **The fourth line of the offer** has not been read out loud. The refusals to read are nine, the departure is unspent, and there is still no date in the ledger for the next reading.
- **A rate that turns a year into coppers.** There is none and there never has been one, and it is restated in Chapters 225, 227, 229 and 230.
- **The two figures of five years and nine years.** Not joined and neither can be got off the other.
- **The boy of nine and his name in a column in a building two days of road away.** Untouched. **A boy of about nine carried four words in this block, the four words are printed on the page, and he was entered as not a bearer and not a clerk and not anybody's instrument, and the two boys are not to be confused.**
- **The romance.** **It did not move in this block and that is deliberate.** Three rooms with a shut door in them and a man going through the door twice, and nothing said in them that a person could be found for. UNRESOLVED, AND THE VOLUME MUST NOT END ON A SETTLEMENT.
- **Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block and nothing required him.**

## Corrections this block made, that a Batch 0004 writer must inherit

- **THE POOL OF REFUSALS WITH NO REASON IS AT EIGHT AND THE REFUSALS ABOUT THE NINTH HOLDING ARE AT SEVEN, AND THE FIGURE OF SIXTEEN IS THE COUNTER MAN'S ORDINAL AND THE NEXT IS SEVENTEEN.** The chain is eleventh on the second, twelfth on the fourth, thirteenth on the sixth, fourteenth on the ninth, fifteenth on the fifteenth, sixteenth on the twenty-third. **No state file may give him a day, because this district has never once given that man a day to be here on.**
- **THE RELATIVE MONTH IS *LAST MONTH* AND IT MEANS THE THIRD MONTH, and it holds after the turn.** From the third of the fifth month onward the second month is *the month before last*; the third month is *last month* until the first of June. **The pool of refusals last moved on the twenty-fourth of last month, which is thirty-seven days before the thirtieth of April, and the six households have not moved since the twenty-first of last month, which is forty days before it.**
- **A CHAPTER MAY CARRY TWO DATES, and Chapter 229 carries the thirtieth of April and the first of the fifth month.** The first time was Chapter 210 with the tenth and the eleventh of April. **A Batch 0004 writer who counts one day to a chapter has ten days for Chapters 231 to 240, the third to the twelfth of the fifth month, and that is exactly one chapter a day.**
- **THE MONTH TURNED ON THE FIRST OF THE FIFTH MONTH AND WAS SAID ON THE SECOND, and the reason it was not said on the morning it turned is on the page.** A Batch 0004 writer must not say it again and must not pretend the turn waited.
- **THE KEEPER'S COLUMN IS STILL HEADED WITH A WORD AND NOT WITH A NAME, and a third column of that shape was found in this block, carrying a man once.** Chapters 213, 218, 220, 225, 226.
- **A SHUT DOOR IS AN INSTRUMENT AND A PAGE IS A DIFFERENT INSTRUMENT, and this block's finding is that the two do not substitute for each other.** A room with a shut door is the only place a person can be told no and have the no be true; a yard is the only place nineteen people can hear him say it; and a page is what a stranger can walk up to, and the Registry's page is four hundred miles away and cannot be carried out of a registrar's own office. **The count of instruments built and not named is six and a shut door, a room and a page were each refused a seventh on the page.**
- **A BLOCK RECORD MAY NOT DEFINE AWAY A FALSE COUNT IN THE PROSE, and a state file may not be the thing that excuses a self-counting character.** The Batch 0002 writing pass broke that rule at its 9.5 and the repair withdrew the defence. **In this block the man of about thirty-one says he has carried a ledger nine or ten times and cannot know it, and the prose leaves the claim open and has a man of fifty-six and a clerk enter that a man counting his own unpaid labour is keeping a tally and not a clerk. That is the shape of the repair and it is the only shape.**
- **TWO RESIDUAL DEFECTS INHERITED FROM BATCH 0002 WERE FOUND AND REPAIRED BY THIS RUN, and both are the class a repair pass creates when it checks the prose and not the rest.** Chapter 214's all-caps footer still read THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF THE MONTH BEFORE LAST because the earlier scan was case-sensitive, and `state/chapter-summaries.md`'s Chapter 0219 entry still read a column headed with a keeper's name because the repair note was written and the entry was not. **A review of this block must scan case-insensitively and must read the state files it claims to have repaired.**

# Open threads after Volume 05 Batch 0004 (Chapters 231 to 240)

**Additive to the section above, which is the position before this block. Anything in that section that this block spends says so here. Anything this block opens is here with the chapter it stands at.**

- **A TOLL SHEET HAS LANDED IN FOUR PLACES THAT NEVER AGREED TO IT, AND TWO PLACES NOW HOLD THE FOURTH OF THE FOUR AND NEITHER CAN BE SHOWN TO BE RIGHT.** The sheet names four parties and not one person and names no place, and it says a place is the fourth of the four by itself until somebody says it is not. This yard decided it was the fourth without being asked, and a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said out loud that he was not going to be the man who decided it. **A man of about forty-one who keeps a road house nine miles up walked down and said his house is the fourth of the four and that his house did not sign.** A clerk entered that two places hold the fourth and that neither can be shown to be right. 231, 233, 240. **OPEN, AND IT IS THE ENGINE OF THE NEXT BLOCK. The nine separate local charters must not become one document with nine places on it, which is the shape of the page out of the cave and the reason its ninth place is empty.**

- **A REGISTRY NOTICE IDENTIFIES THIS DISTRICT'S RECORDS OFFICE AS A RIVAL RECORD AND TAKES EFFECT ON THE FIRST DAY OF THE NEXT MONTH.** It names no place and no person. It identifies an office by what it does and where its books are, and the only line in it that has anything to do with the registrar is her own rule of the twenty-first of the fourth month, which she wrote before she knew there was anything on it and which is the only reason the notice could be written. **A rival record is asked against, and the body will ask it against four places and two of which have not answered.** The notice has been read out five times in seven days in a yard a stranger can walk into, and a clerk entered at the end that this district has made it its own without anybody deciding to. 234, 235, 237, 238, 240. **OPEN, AND IT IS THE HARDEST DEADLINE IN THE VOLUME: TWENTY DAYS AFTER THE TWELFTH OF THE FIFTH MONTH.**

- **THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST READ A LOCK TWICE IN FRONT OF WITNESSES, WAS WRONG BOTH TIMES, AND SAID SO OUT LOUD BOTH TIMES IN HIS OWN WORDS.** The pin takes nothing and holds nothing except the screw's own turning, and what holds a channel is the frame and the weight of the water sitting on it. The notch in a sill is where the board goes and is not a mark of how high the water got. **A man of fifty-six was right the first time and a man of about forty-one was right the second, and each of them is right out of nine years of his own body's work.** 232, 236. **SPENT. DO NOT SPEND IT AGAIN.**

- **A LOCK IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IN THIS BASIN THAT HAS NEVER NEEDED A PERSON, AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT HAS EVER BEEN TO LOOK AT ONE OF THE NINE.** Nine structures in the water above four districts have been standing open on eight of eleven nights, one a night, since the first of April, and the ninth of the printed nine was the twentieth of April, and this district cannot say whether that night happened because nobody has been. **A man of about thirty-four who digs loam said out loud that he could have walked to one of them in a morning and did not, and a clerk entered that a thing a man could have done and did not do is not a refusal and is not a consent and is not a count.** 232, 238. **OPEN, AND IT IS WHAT THE NEXT BLOCK IS FOR.**

- **THE ROMANCE MOVED TWICE, IN A ROOM AND IN ABOUT NINE FEET OF LANE, AND IS NOT RESOLVED.** In a room on the ninth he put both of his errors on her table and she asked him one question that was not a form question. In about nine feet of lane at about twenty to five on the eleventh he said out loud the fortnight of silence about a question he asked her on the twenty-fourth of last month and was refused in four seconds, and she said he did it on purpose and would not have forgiven him for not doing it, and he asked the other half of it, and she said she would come at about half past ten on the twelfth. **She came, and the last exchange between them is three lines long and one of them is a man naming what he did not do.** 237, 239, 240. **OPEN, AND THE VOLUME MUST NOT END ON A SETTLEMENT. A slate goes past a kitchen for the twelfth time in the next block and one of them writes on it and it is not about the water. The slate is untouched in this block.**

- **A SEVENTH INSTRUMENT WAS PROPOSED AND REFUSED AND HAS NOT BEEN PROPOSED AGAIN.** A man saying out loud in front of other people that the thing he knows best in the world is not what he said it was. **The man who proposed it said out loud on the twelfth that a rule about being wrong is the same shape of rule as the sentence one of the nine wrote under NO PERSON SHALL HOLD THIS ALONE four hundred years ago, and a clerk entered that saying so is not withdrawing it and the six are still six.** 232, 236, 240. **OPEN, AND IT IS THE BLOCK'S GIFT TO THE VOLUME'S ARGUMENT. THE NEXT BLOCK SHOULD NOT SPEND IT BY WRITING THE RULE DOWN.**

- **A MAN WALKED TWO HUNDRED YARDS AND ASKED ONE MAN ONE QUESTION IN HIS OWN DOORWAY, AFTER ASKING THE WOMAN WHO CARRIES THE WATER FOR THAT HOUSE FIRST.** It was the first question anybody had put to that man in nine years, and he refused a toll with a reason and said he would answer a second. **A man of fifty-six said that a man who has been asked one question in nine years and has answered it is the only instrument this basin has that the four districts have not built yet, and that a yard which starts asking one question in one doorway is not a yard that is going to ask nine questions in nine doorways this week.** 239. **OPEN, AND IT IS THE FIRST OF THE NINE SEPARATE THINGS.**

- **THE LEDGER WENT BACK TWO HUNDRED YARDS ON A MORNING WHEN THE MAN WHO KEEPS THAT COUNTER WAS WELL, AFTER FIFTEEN DAYS ON A TABLE FOUR FEET HIGH UNDER A HATCH, AND NOBODY HAD ASKED HIM.** A person who has not been asked to carry a thing and has carried it has not refused it and has not consented to it, and the record about the fourteen days before that morning says not asked. 235. **OPEN, AND THE WORK DID NOT STOP, IT WENT SOMEWHERE ELSE AGAIN.**

- **THE BORROWED WORD CAME BACK ON SOMEBODY ELSE'S PAPER AND A MAN NAMED IT OUT LOUD BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED.** A toll sheet printed the word four times on the third of the fifth month and he distinguished it from the gate at the end of that yard in the same sentence. 231. **OPEN, AND THE PROSE MAY NOT GO BACK TO IT WITHOUT THE DISTRICT NOTICING.**

- **THE TANK FOUR MILES DOWN A LANE IS OVER ITS LIP, THE CRACK IS ABOUT THE LENGTH OF A HAND, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED FOR A FIFTH MEND.** Two chalk lines on the inside of the brick, nine years of beds in the second room, and a man of fifty-five who refused again on the seventh and sent nothing on the tenth. 235, 238. **OPEN, AND IT IS NOT JOINED TO THE NOTCH IN A SILL, AND THAT REFUSAL WAS MADE ON THE EIGHTH WHILE A MAN WAS STANDING NEXT TO BOTH OF THEM.**

- **A CHAIR HAS STOOD AT THE END OF THE TRESTLE TABLE FOR NINETEEN DAYS AND NOBODY HAS SAT IN IT.** 231, 235, 237. **OPEN, AND IT IS A PIECE OF FURNITURE AND NOT A BEARER AND NOT A FOUNDER AND IT DID NOT BECOME A FOUNDER BY STANDING STILL.**

- **THE NINTH PLACE ON THE PAGE IN THE CAVE IS RULED AND EMPTY AND WAS NAMED AS RULED AND EMPTY IN CHAPTERS 231, 232 AND 240, AND NOBODY FILLED IT.** OPEN AND LOAD-BEARING. **THE NINTH NAME IS NOT ON THE PAGE AND IS THE VOLUME'S NEXT QUESTION AND BELONGS TO IVEN TALLOW, WHO IS NOT IN THE VOLUME, DOES NOT APPEAR, AND IS NOT TO BE NAMED OR FORESHADOWED.**

- **THE POOL OF REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN STOOD AT EIGHT AGAINST FOUR SEPARATE ATTEMPTS TO MOVE IT IN THIS BLOCK.** Four people refused something with a reason and not one of them is a clerk of a house: the man of about forty-one about a toll his house has not signed for, the woman of fifty-eight about her two buckets, the man of about forty-four at the ninth of the eleven houses, and the man of about twenty-three about a copy and a picture. 231, 233, 235, 239. **OPEN, AND A NINTH MUST NOT BE ENTERED WITHOUT A REASON ON THE PAGE. The refusals about the ninth holding are at SEVEN. The man of about thirty at a counter is at the sixteenth time and the next is the seventeenth and was not in this district on any of the ten days and no chapter gave him a day.**

## Carried forward unspent from earlier batches, and untouched in this block

- **The nine local charters, the regional covenant, the Council's letter calling this district's man a founder on nine walls, and the Convener promotion.** Not in this block. Chapters 241 onward.
- **Cael Orin and the man the figure of twenty-one years is against have not met and did not meet in this block, and Orin's name was not said a second or a third time.**
- **The ninth design signature belongs to Iven Tallow and Iven is not in this volume.** Untouched and not foreshadowed.
- **The office** — five places, no register, two valid certificates in a drawer thirty-four miles away. **Not approached in any of the ten chapters.**
- **The fourth line of the offer** has not been read out loud. The refusals to read are nine, the departure is unspent, and there is still no date in the ledger for the next reading.
- **A rate that turns a year into coppers.** There is none and there never has been one.
- **The two figures of five years and nine years.** Not joined and neither can be got off the other.
- **The boy of nine and his name in a column in a building two days of road away.** Untouched. **A boy of about nine carried four words in Batch 0003 and is not in this block.**
- **The lease.** Unanswerable, and the man with the portfolio is not in this district.
- **Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block.**

## Corrections this block made, that a Batch 0005 writer must inherit

- **THE RELATIVE MONTH IS *LAST MONTH* AND IT MEANS THE FOURTH MONTH, AND *THE MONTH BEFORE LAST* IS THE THIRD.** The pool of refusals last moved on the twenty-fourth of **the month before last**, which is forty-nine days before the twenty-second of the fifth month, and that phrase must not be written as *last month*.
- **THE MONTH TURNED IN THE NIGHT OF THE THIRTIETH OF APRIL, WAS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE ON THE SECOND OF THE FIFTH MONTH, AND WAS NOT SAID AGAIN IN CHAPTERS 231 TO 240.** The phrase *the month turned* appears nowhere in that block.
- **NO WEEKDAY IS ATTACHED TO ANY DATE AND NO WEEKDAY NAME IS SPOKEN IN CHAPTERS 231 TO 240.** Batch 0002 used the pattern in four chapters, Batch 0003 in one, and this block in none. **Five weekday words were in the first draft and all five were removed by a case-insensitive whole-word scan.**
- **A CHAPTER MAY CARRY TWO DATES AND THIS BLOCK'S CHAPTERS DO NOT.** Ten days, ten chapters, one a day, from the third to the twelfth of the fifth month.
- **THE KEEPER'S COLUMN IS STILL HEADED WITH A WORD AND NOT WITH A NAME, AND A THIRD COLUMN OF THAT SHAPE WAS FOUND IN BATCH 0003 CARRYING A MAN ONCE.** Chapters 213, 218, 220, 225, 226, and nothing in this block.
- **A SHUT DOOR AND ABOUT NINE FEET OF LANE ARE TWO DIFFERENT PLACES, AND NEITHER SUBSTITUTES FOR A PAGE.** A room with a shut door is where a sentence goes to die and not a book; about nine feet of lane is not that and is not a yard either; a doorway is the fourth place this district found this month and it is the only one anybody used on a man who had never been asked a question in nine years.
- **A BLOCK RECORD MAY NOT DEFINE AWAY A FALSE COUNT IN THE PROSE, AND A RUNNING FIGURE IN PROSE MUST BE RE-DERIVED WHEN A CHAPTER IS REVISED.** Two figures in Chapter 240's footer were on the footer and not in the body and were put into the body. The tally of how many times the notice has been read out was three different figures in three chapters and is now one. **A review of this block must run a case-insensitive whole-word reserved-term scan, a footer-support check that searches the body of its own chapter for every number phrase in the footer, and a running-figure check on any number that recurs across chapters.**

# Open threads after Volume 05 Batch 0005 (Chapters 241 to 250)

**Additive to the sections above. THIS IS THE LAST BLOCK OF VOLUME 05. There is no Chapter 251 and no Batch 0006, and everything in this section is either paid, or reserved, or open at the volume close and named here as such.**

- **NINE LOCAL CHARTERS STAND AROUND ONE WATERSHED, EACH WITH ITS OWN BEARERS, ITS OWN TERM AND ITS OWN RIGHT TO WITHDRAW, AND NONE OF THEM IS ON ANY OTHER'S SHEET. THE NINE LOCKS STAY FINITE AND NOBODY OWNS THE WATER.** Four terms that are not the same: five seasons, three, five, and one season with a man of about seventy-one's reason for it. 244, 245, 250. **PAID. The volume's central promise is on the page.**

- **A GIVING UP DOES NOT GIVE BACK A SEASON, AND THE BLOCK SAYS WHAT IT COSTS ON THE PAGE, IN TWO SPEECHES, BY A MAN WHO IS NOT ONE OF THE FIVE, AND DOES NOT ASK THE PERSON WHO WOULD PAY IT.** 244. **PAID. The cost is about four hundred yards of drain and two other men's backs and a hole in the middle of the arrangement, and the spending is not priced because there is no rate in this basin that turns a year into coppers.**

- **THE FIRST REGIONAL PUBLIC COVENANT IN THIS BASIN, ADOPTED IN PUBLIC IN A BARN BY SHOW OF HANDS, HAND-COPIED, READ OUT AT THREE OF THE FOUR COUNTERS, AND ENTERED IN A BOOK A STRANGER MAY WALK UP TO, WITH THE HOLE IN IT ON ITS FACE.** 246, 247, 250. **PAID. Four stewards, a term, public tolls, an appeal path into another region, and no person shall hold this alone on the second line.**

- **THE THIRD OF THE COVENANT'S THREE COLUMNS IS HEADED BY A WORD AND NOT BY A NAME AND IS RULED AND EMPTY ON ITS FACE, AND THE SHEET FORBIDS FILLING IT AND FORBIDS ASKING A HOUSE ABOUT A PAGE IT WAS ALLOWED TO COPY.** 246. **PAID, AND IT IS THE STRUCTURE THE VOLUME HAS BEEN BUILDING SINCE CHAPTER 213. The next one of that shape is not to be found by going to a house. OPEN IN THE SENSE THAT IT IS PERMANENTLY EMPTY, AND THAT IS THE POINT.**

- **HE IS A CONVENER AND HIS NAME IS A PUBLIC INDEX, AND A STRANGER CAME FOUR MILES TO A GATE AND ASKED FOR HIM BY NAME, AND A NAME PUT ON A FIGURE CANNOT BE TAKEN OFF IT BY THE PERSON IT IS PUT ON AND HE SAID THAT HIMSELF BEFORE HE TOOK IT.** 248. **PAID, AND IT COST HIM THE LAST PRIVATE THING HE HAD. A man of fifty-six said in the yard that four strangers in four months is not a market and that he was not going to help this yard get built out of a name.**

- **THE COUNCIL LOST THE IMMEDIATE VOTE AND CALLED HIM A FOUNDER IN A LETTER THAT WENT OUT ON NINE WALLS, AND THE LETTER CANNOT BE ANSWERED BY A MAN IN A YARD. A WALL IS NOT AN ADDRESS.** 247, 249. **PAID, AND THE COUNCIL HAS NO FACE IN THREE BLOCKS. The word founder is on a piece of paper and on nine walls and was not said back by anybody in that yard, and a clerk entered that a man who refuses to deny a word does not thereby claim it.**

- **A LOCK IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IN THIS BASIN THAT HAS NEVER NEEDED A PERSON, AND ON THE FIFTEENTH OF THE FIFTH MONTH ABOUT NINE PEOPLE WALKED TO ONE OF THE NINE AND FOUND THE BOARD IN THE NOTCH.** 243. **SPENT, AND IT WAS SPENT BY WALKING AND NOT BY A PANEL. This district can now say the board is in, the water is about a foot over it, the road below is shut, and nobody is holding it, and it still cannot say whether the ninth of the nine printed nights happened on the twentieth of last month, because a plank does not keep nights. THE NINTH OF THE NINE NIGHTS REMAINS OPEN AND IS NOT TO BE CLOSED BY ANYBODY.**

- **THE SINGLE-HOLDER SAFEGUARD IS SHOWN ON THE PAGE TO HAVE BEEN DESIGNED TO DISTRIBUTE POWER AND NOT TO CROWN ANYBODY, AND ONE OF THE NINE WROTE A RULE ABOUT BEING THANKED FOUR HUNDRED YEARS AGO UNDERLINED TWICE.** 246. **PAID AS FAR AS IT CAN BE PAID WITHOUT THE NINTH NAME. THE NINTH DESIGN SIGNATURE IS NOT ON THE PAGE, IS NOT IN THIS VOLUME, DOES NOT APPEAR, AND IS NOT TO BE NAMED OR FORESHADOWED BY THE VOLUME CLOSE EITHER.**

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE WHO KEEPS A ROAD HOUSE AT THE TOP OF THE ROAD SAID THAT SOMEBODY COMES DOWN IN MARCH AND PUTS THE BOARD IN AND GOES AGAIN, AND THAT HE DOES NOT KNOW WHAT HE IS CALLED AND HAS NOT ASKED, AND THAT THE DAY HE ASKS IS THE DAY THERE IS A MAN AT THE TOP OF THAT ROAD INSTEAD OF A HOUSE.** 243. **OPEN, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN THIS VOLUME THAT A KEEPER HAS BEEN NEARLY DESCRIBED, AND NO KEEPER IS NAMED AND NO KEEPER IS ON A PAGE AND THERE NEVER WAS ONE. HE HAS NO DAY IN THIS DISTRICT AND NONE MAY BE GIVEN TO HIM.**

- **THE ROMANCE MOVED TWICE IN THIS BLOCK AND IS NOT RESOLVED, AND THE VOLUME DOES NOT END ON A SETTLEMENT.** 242, in a yard, she says out loud that the answer a man gave in a doorway is not hers; 250, a slate goes past a kitchen for the twelfth time and one of them writes on it and it is not about the water, he does not read it out, she says that being fair about one thing without being told that it is not fair is convenient, and the volume ends about two hours later on a man in a channel with his hand on nothing. **OPEN, AND THE NEXT VOLUME OPENS ON IT.**

- **THE NOTICE TAKES EFFECT ON THE FIRST DAY OF THE NEXT MONTH, WHICH IS TEN DAYS AFTER THE LAST DAY OF THE VOLUME'S PROSE.** 234, 237, 240, 246, 249. **OPEN AND UNANSWERED. It has been read out seven times in seventeen days in a yard a stranger can walk into and this district has made it its own twice over and is not going to answer it by carrying a page out of a registrar's own office. A registrar could not finish reading it on the twenty-first of the fifth month.**

- **THE NINTH CHARTER HAS NO NAMES UNDER THE LINE AND THE RECORD SAYS NOT ASKED.** 245. **OPEN, PERMANENTLY, AND IT IS DELIBERATE: a block that filled the ninth line would have produced the shape of the page out of the cave on a second document. THE VOLUME CLOSE MUST DECIDE WHETHER A VOLUME MAY END WITH A NINTH THING UNWRITTEN AND MUST SAY SO PLAINLY RATHER THAN REPAIR IT.**

- **THIS DISTRICT HAS FOUR DIFFERENT NINTHS IN IT AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT THEY ARE FOUR AND ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER AND ARE NOT A PATTERN**, being the ninth of the nine charters, the ninth of the eleven houses the woman of fifty-eight carries water to, the ninth of the nine printed nights, and the ninth place on the page out of the cave. 250. **OPEN, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THE VOLUME HAS SAID OUT LOUD THAT IT WAS COLLECTING NINTHS.**

- **A TANK FOUR MILES DOWN A LANE IS OVER ITS LIP, THE CRACK IS ABOUT THE LENGTH OF A HAND, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED FOR A FIFTH MEND.** 246, 250. **OPEN, AND IT WAS USED TWICE IN THIS BLOCK AS A THING THAT IS NOT A SIXTH AND WAS NOT JOINED TO THE FIRST CHARTER, TO THE NOTCH IN A SILL OR TO ANYTHING ELSE.**

- **A MAN WALKED TWO HUNDRED YARDS AND ASKED ONE MAN ONE QUESTION IN HIS OWN DOORWAY, AND A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FOUR THEN WALKED TWO HUNDRED YARDS DOWN BEHIND THE BANK ON HIS OWN TWO FEET AND WAS ASKED A SECOND ONE, AND HE PUT HIS OWN NAME UNDER A LINE IN HIS OWN ROOM.** 239, 241. **PAID, AND IT IS THE FIRST OF THE NINE.**

- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHOSE NAME IS ON A PAGE AGAINST THIS DISTRICT'S RECORDS OFFICE.** Untouched in this block and now load-bearing for a second person, because the same rule was used on the man of twenty-one years in Chapter 248. **OPEN.**

## Carried forward unspent from every batch of Volume 05, and untouched in this block

- **Cael Orin and the man the figure of twenty-one years is against have not met.** The lease is unanswerable and the man with the portfolio is not in this district.
- **The office** — five places, no register, two valid certificates in a drawer thirty-four miles away. Not approached.
- **The fourth line of the offer** has not been read out loud. The refusals to read are nine, the departure is unspent, and there is still no date in the ledger for the next reading.
- **A rate that turns a year into coppers.** There is none and there never has been one, and it is printed on two of the three documents of this block.
- **The two figures of five years and nine years.** Not joined and neither can be got off the other.
- **The boy of nine and his name in a column in a building two days of road away.** Untouched.
- **The blank lot, the origin of the Common Measure, the First House, the removed signature, the three unexplained things of Volume 01, the iron, the echo of an origin toll, the Last Fare, the market stalls on the lower terrace, the first channel's failure and its sills, the four gaps, the two impressions, the two lines holding the office's name, the four lines of the broken circle, the signal's memory pattern, the pan that cannot report itself, the place that cannot report itself, and the mark on the iron.** All untouched.
- **Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block.**

## Corrections this block made, that a volume close must inherit

- **THE RELATIVE MONTH IS *LAST MONTH* AND IT MEANS THE FOURTH MONTH, AND *THE MONTH BEFORE LAST* IS THE THIRD.** The pool of refusals last moved on the twenty-fourth of **the month before last**, which is fifty days before the thirteenth of the fifth month and fifty-nine before the twenty-second, and that phrase must not be written as *last month*.
- **THE MONTH TURNED IN THE NIGHT OF THE THIRTIETH OF APRIL, WAS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE ON THE SECOND OF THE FIFTH MONTH, AND WAS NOT SAID AGAIN IN CHAPTERS 231 TO 250.** The phrase *the month turned* appears nowhere in those twenty chapters.
- **NO WEEKDAY IS ATTACHED TO ANY DATE AND NO WEEKDAY NAME IS SPOKEN IN CHAPTERS 231 TO 250.** Batch 0002 used the pattern in four chapters, Batch 0003 in one, Batch 0004 in none, and Batch 0005 in none. **Six weekday names were in the first draft of this block and all six were removed by a case-insensitive whole-word scan.**
- **NO CHAPTER IN CHAPTERS 231 TO 250 CARRIES TWO DATES.** Twenty chapters, twenty days, one chapter a day.
- **THE DISTRICT'S OWN NAME APPEARS NOWHERE IN CHAPTERS 231 TO 250.** A scan for *Alder Reach* returns zero in those twenty chapters and one hit in the whole of the volume, in Chapter 204, which is closed canon.
- **A KEEPER HAS STILL NEVER BEEN ON A PAGE AND THERE NEVER WAS ONE, AND NO KEEPER IS NAMED.** The March man in Chapter 243 is described by what a man of about forty-one does not know about him.
- **A REVIEW MUST RUN FOUR CHECKS AND ALL FOUR ARE NAMED IN THE BLOCK RECORD AT SECTION 7:** a class-one and class-two counting-motif detector calibrated on Chapters 221 to 240; a footer-support check that searches the body of its own chapter for every capitalised phrase and every numeral in the footer; a running-figure check on any figure that recurs across chapters, and there were **four running figures in this block** — the notice readings, the days on a board, the days the chair has stood and the days the six households have not moved; and a formulaic-construction measure with its method and its denominator printed in full beside the table. **The block's own instrument phrase rose from three sentence-openings in ten chapters to seven, and the disclosure is the point.**

# Open threads at the Volume 05 close

**Additive to every section above. VOLUME 05 IS CLOSED AT CHAPTER 250. There is no Chapter 251 and there is no Batch 0006, and this phase created neither. Every thread in this section is OPEN and is handed to the Volume 06 open as it stands. The close resolved nothing, and where the close repaired a figure it repaired it against the chapters and did not close a thread.**

**THE RECORD OF THE CLOSE IS `state/volume-05-close.md` AND THE DOCUMENT A VOLUME 06 WRITER OPENS WITH IS `state/volume-05-roll-summary.md`. Both were written after the fifty chapters and both were measured against them.**

## Open and handed forward, each with the chapter it stands at

- **NINE LOCAL CHARTERS IN NINE ROOMS, AND THE NINTH OF THEM HAS NO NAMES UNDER THE LINE BECAUSE THE PEOPLE AT THAT PLACE HAVE NOT BEEN ASKED.** 244, 245, 250. **OPEN, PERMANENTLY, AND DELIBERATELY. The Volume 05 close was asked to decide whether a volume may end with a ninth thing unwritten. IT MAY, AND IT DOES, AND A BLOCK THAT FILLED THE NINTH LINE WOULD HAVE PRODUCED THE SHAPE OF THE PAGE OUT OF THE CAVE ON A SECOND DOCUMENT.**
- **THE THIRD OF THE COVENANT'S THREE COLUMNS IS RULED AND EMPTY ON THE FACE OF THE SHEET, AND THE SHEET FORBIDS FILLING IT AND FORBIDS ASKING A HOUSE ABOUT A PAGE IT WAS ALLOWED TO COPY.** 246, 250. **OPEN IN THE SENSE THAT IT IS PERMANENTLY EMPTY, AND THAT IS THE POINT. THE NEXT ONE OF THAT SHAPE IS NOT TO BE FOUND BY GOING TO A HOUSE.**
- **THE NINTH OF THE NINE NIGHTS.** The ninth was the twentieth of April. 243, and 250. **OPEN AND NOT TO BE CLOSED BY ANYBODY. This district walked nine miles to one of the nine on the fifteenth of the fifth month, found the board in the notch, and cannot say whether that night happened, because a plank does not keep nights.**
- **THE REGISTRY'S NOTICE TAKES EFFECT ON THE FIRST DAY OF THE NEXT MONTH, WHICH IS TEN DAYS AFTER THE LAST DAY OF THE VOLUME'S PROSE.** 234, 237, 240, 246, 249, 250. **OPEN AND UNANSWERED. Read out seven times in seventeen days in a yard a stranger can walk into. A registrar could not finish reading it on the twenty-first of the fifth month. This district has made it its own twice over and is not going to answer it by carrying a page out of her own office.**
- **HE IS A CONVENER AND HIS NAME IS A PUBLIC INDEX, AND A NAME PUT ON A FIGURE CANNOT BE TAKEN OFF IT BY THE PERSON IT IS PUT ON.** 248, 249, 250. **PAID AND IT COST HIM THE LAST PRIVATE THING HE HAD. THE CONSEQUENCE CARRIES FORWARD AND IS NOT SPENT: a man who can convene nine settlements and cannot be convened out of a column is a man a founder's mark can reach through a claim, and he knows it and said so before anybody put him in the column.**
- **THE COUNCIL HAS NO FACE AND ACQUIRED NONE IN FOUR BLOCKS. A WALL IS NOT AN ADDRESS AND THE LETTER ON NINE WALLS CANNOT BE ANSWERED BY A MAN IN A YARD.** 247, 249. **THE WORD FOUNDER IS ON A PIECE OF PAPER AND ON NINE WALLS AND WAS NOT SAID BACK BY ANYBODY IN THAT YARD, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A MAN WHO REFUSES TO DENY A WORD DOES NOT THEREBY CLAIM IT AND THAT NOTHING ANYBODY SAID THAT AFTERNOON TOOK THE NAME OFF HIM EITHER. PAID, AND THE WORD IS STILL ON THE WALLS.**
- **THE SINGLE-HOLDER SAFEGUARD IS SHOWN TO HAVE BEEN WRITTEN TO DISTRIBUTE POWER AND NOT TO CROWN ANYBODY, AND ONE OF THE NINE WROTE A RULE ABOUT BEING THANKED FOUR HUNDRED YEARS AGO, UNDERLINED TWICE.** 246. **PAID AS FAR AS IT CAN BE PAID WITHOUT THE NINTH NAME. THE NINTH DESIGN SIGNATURE IS NOT ON THE PAGE, IS NOT IN THE VOLUME, DOES NOT APPEAR, AND IS NOT TO BE NAMED OR FORESHADOWED BY THE VOLUME 06 OPEN EITHER.**
- **THE ROMANCE MOVED SIX TIMES IN FIFTY DAYS AND IS NOT RESOLVED, AND THE VOLUME DID NOT END ON A SETTLEMENT.** 201 to 210, 217, 221 to 230, 237, 239, 242, 250. **OPEN, AND THE NEXT VOLUME OPENS ON IT. A MOVEMENT IS NOT A RESOLUTION AND A FRACTURE IS NOT A SETTLEMENT. The last exchange in the volume is a woman saying that being fair about one thing without being told that it is not fair is convenient, and the volume ends about two hours later on a man in a channel.**
- **THE OFFICE.** Five places, no register, two valid certificates in a drawer thirty-four miles away, eleven trades that cannot state its function, four gaps in four books in four rooms, four documents that mention no office, one sentence in the column for things that are not findings, the question in a public book dated the fifth of July. **NOT APPROACHED IN ANY OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS. THE CLOSE CHECKED AND IT IS NOT SETTLED.**
- **THE FOURTH LINE OF THE OFFER.** On a table five times, never accepted and never refused, and **not read out loud anywhere in Volume 05.** Restated in 249 and not read. **THE REFUSALS TO READ ARE NINE, THE DEPARTURE IS UNSPENT, AND THERE IS STILL NO DATE IN THE LEDGER FOR THE NEXT READING. NOT SPENT.**
- **CAEL ORIN AND THE LEASE.** His name was said out loud once in a yard in Chapter 203 and was not said a second time in Chapters 204 to 250. The lease is unanswerable, the place is not named on the copy, the man with the portfolio is not in this district, **and they have never met.**
- **THE NAME OF THE CITY ON THE AGENT'S LEDGER, AND THE NAME OF THE AGENT IN ANY REGISTER IN THE WEST BANK.** Neither is given. No place is named on any document in this district and none is named in narration.
- **THE BLANK LOT, IVEN TALLOW, THE ORIGIN OF THE COMMON MEASURE, THE FIRST HOUSE, THE REMOVED SIGNATURE, THE THREE UNEXPLAINED THINGS OF VOLUME 01.** All untouched. A whole-word scan of the fifty chapters returns zero for every one of them.
- **A WAY OF SAYING WHERE A TOLL LANDS ON A PLACE.** The fifth of the five things this district does not have, still unnamed and unpriced, with five boards, six lines full and a seventh ruled and empty. Eleven coppers a week asked three times in the last block and refused three times; the district has said no seven times in the volume.
- **A RATE THAT TURNS A YEAR INTO COPPERS.** There is none and there never has been one, **and the sentence saying so is printed on two of the three documents the volume made.**
- **THE IRON, THE ECHO OF AN ORIGIN TOLL, AND THE LAST FARE.** All three untouched, unread and unreferred to in any of the fifty chapters. **The two occurrences of the words *the iron*, at Chapters 232 and 240, are the cast iron of a lock frame and of a channel pin, and neither is the iron on the bench.**
- **A KEEPER.** No keeper has ever been on a page and there never was one, and no keeper is named. **The volume came within one sentence of naming one in Chapter 243 and stopped.** The March man is described by what a man of about forty-one does not know about him and by nothing else.
- **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHOSE NAME IS ON A PAGE AGAINST THIS DISTRICT'S RECORDS OFFICE, AND A NAME PUT ON A FIGURE CANNOT BE TAKEN OFF IT BY THE MAN IT IS PUT ON.** Untouched in the last block and **now load-bearing for a second person, because the same rule was used on the man of twenty-one years in Chapter 248. OPEN.**
- **THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS CAME OFF THIS DISTRICT'S OWN PAGE ON THE THIRTY-FIRST OF THE THIRD MONTH, IN THE SAME LINE AS THE DATE, AND NOTHING WAS WRITTEN IN ITS PLACE.** Open, and a figure that has not been established is not a blank, it is the absence of one.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE WHO KEEPS A ROAD HOUSE AT THE TOP OF THE ROAD.** He has no day in this district, this district has never once given him one, and **none may be given to him.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER.** The sixteenth time down the eleven mile road; the next is the seventeenth; **no chapter in fifty has ever given him a day in this district.**
- **A TANK FOUR MILES DOWN A LANE IS OVER ITS LIP, THE CRACK IS ABOUT THE LENGTH OF A HAND, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED FOR A FIFTH MEND IN FOUR MONTHS.** Open, and it was used twice in the last block as a thing that is not a sixth protected thing.
- **FOUR EMPTY HOUSES OFFERED TO THE YARD AND NOT TAKEN, AND A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE WHO SAID NO BEFORE HE WAS ASKED.** Open, and his four houses were not added to the eleven of the woman of fifty-eight.
- **SIX HOUSEHOLDS THAT HAVE NOT MOVED SINCE THE TWENTY-ONE OF APRIL.** One signed, two refused, three not asked again. Thirty-one days at the last day of the volume, derived.
- **A CHAIR AT THE END OF THE TRESTLE TABLE, PUT DOWN ON THE TWENTY-THIRD OF APRIL AND MOVED FOUR FEET ONCE.** Twenty-nine days at the last day of the volume and **nobody has sat in it on any of the twenty-nine.**
- **THE ELEVEN COULD NOT HAVE TAKEN THE WORD OFF THE PAGE BY DENYING IT, AND THE DENIAL WOULD HAVE PUT IT IN A COLUMN.** Open, and it is the shape of everything this volume has done.
- **A SLATE WENT PAST A KITCHEN FOR THE TWELFTH TIME AND CAME BACK WITH TWO LINES ON IT THAT WERE NOT ABOUT THE WATER, AND NO CLERK HAS WRITTEN DOWN ON WHICH DAYS ANY OF THE ELEVEN WENT PAST, AND THAT WAS NOT REPAIRED.** Open, and the record came from one of the two of them and not from the other.

## Carried forward from Volume 04 and still open

- **A man walking about ninety houses a round whose end is nine days and a half, the eleven houses he still carries, and the four of the six things offered and refused.** Untouched in the last block.
- **The four gaps in four books in four rooms, the two impressions, the two lines holding the office's name, the four lines of the broken circle, the market stalls on the lower terrace, the first channel's failure and its sills, the signal's memory pattern, the pan that cannot report itself, the place that cannot report itself, and the mark on the iron.** All untouched.
- **A page in a cave with nine places on it, eight names and one rule saying no person shall hold it alone, and under the rule, in a second hand, a man thanked for it has been made something else and the something is not a chair.** The ninth place is ruled and empty and **was not filled in any of the fifty chapters and is not to be filled by the Volume 06 open.**

## What the close repaired, and what it did not

**Twelve prose repairs across eight chapters and eleven file repairs, all named with file, line and reason in `state/volume-05-close.md` section 5. Two character descriptors carried two ages and now carry one each. One printed document carried three different figures and now carries one. One chapter's footer named a yard its body never named. One class-one claim had no printed paragraph under it and now has one. One garbled figure on the last page of the volume now reads two hundred and six. Four meta phrases in closed prose are now in-world spans. One place name in narration is now the volume's own phrase.**

**NOTHING WAS CLOSED. NO THREAD WAS RESOLVED. THE NINTH OF THE NINE NIGHTS, THE NINTH CHARTER'S EMPTY LINE, THE NOTICE THAT TAKES EFFECT TEN DAYS AFTER THE LAST DAY OF THE PROSE, THE OFFICE, THE FOURTH LINE OF THE OFFER, THE DEPARTURE, THE CITY, THE AGENT, THE BLANK LOT AND IVEN ALL GO FORWARD UNTOUCHED, AND THE CLOSE WAS NOT PERMITTED TO TOUCH ANY OF THEM.**
